# PLAN — the single source of truth (projects · tasks · ETAs)

**This file is THE plan.** Every task lives here, under a project, with an `ETA` date and a `status`.
The long-horizon plan (the 6/22 launch) lives here too — as dated tasks, not a separate doc. There is no
second plan surface: the calendar is phone reminders only, the harness #-task list is a session scratch
mirror, `CHANGELOG.md`/git hold history. If it's planned, it's a line here.

**Line format** (strict — parsed by `scripts/check_plan.py`):
`- #<id> | <YYYY-MM-DD ETA> | <status> | <title>`   ·  status ∈ pending · in_progress · blocked

**The two rituals operate ONLY on this file:**
- **OPEN** (every session): run `python scripts/check_plan.py --today` → it prints OVERDUE + due-today open
  tasks. That list IS the day's plan. State it before reacting to anything.
- **CLOSE** (every session): every open task's ETA must be ≥ today. Rebump any past-due one to a real
  future date (or close it). File every new item as a line here under a project. Then commit — the
  pre-commit gate (`check_plan.py`) REFUSES the commit if any open task lacks a project, an ETA, or has a
  past ETA, or if an open harness task is missing from this file. No gaps, mechanically.

_Last CLOSE: 2026-06-16._

---

We treat volume as positive-only. **(6) THE EXIT IS A GRADED SEQUENCE, NOT A LEVEL** — extended above the 10-DMA + big-volume negative reversal → partials; closing below 23-EMA/50-DMA → sell some or all; volume expansion on the first close below the 23-EMA since breakout → run over. **Three signals, none an R multiple.** ▶ 📊 **AND THE FUNDAMENTALS STRIP CONTRADICTS OUR RUBRIC: Q1-2020 EPS FELL 29% and AMZN was still a model-book leader** (revenue 21→26→40→37%, EPS −29→+97→+192%). **A single weak EPS quarter did not disqualify it — our axis-2 EPS trajectory would have marked it down.** ⚠ **ONE page, one name, and a winner chosen FOR a model book — survivorship by construction. THE PRE-DECLARED MEASURE FAILS, AND IT FAILS INFORMATIVELY.** ▶ **Declared IN THE PROBE BEFORE any number was computed** (`scripts/probes/_594_multiyear_clearance.py`): `clearance_pct` = the % of daily HIGHS in the prior 5 years that the GAP-DAY CLOSE exceeds. What he is judging is the QUALITY AND SHAPE of the base being cleared, not the fraction cleared.** ▶ ⚠ **POST-HOC OBSERVATION, FLAGGED AS SUCH AND NOT A FINDING — I noticed it in the output, it was NOT declared, and reporting it as evidence would be the exact trap I wrote into this line yesterday: the approved names have **1,224** prior bars at median (minimum 1,146) while the BAD names have **655** (minimum 150), and **7 of 17 bad names have under 400 bars versus 0 of 6 approved**. i.e. many of his "garbage" calls are recent listings with no multi-year base to speak of. But the first structural measure we could finally compute does not separate his labels.** >> ⚠ **2026-09-06 — THE BACKFILL INTRODUCED A MONEY-PATH LATENCY REGRESSION AND I CAUGHT IT BEFORE PARKING. Found while checking whether his "delayed entry with EP day close reclaim" idea already existed — it did, as one of four patterns. ▶ **WHAT IT IS:** every name the EP scan sees joins on its EP day and is watched 20 sessions; `delayed_entry_shadow.py` records raw path facts and fires four patterns, each with its own buy AND stop: **`ep_low_reclaim`** (undercut the EP-day low, 5-min close back above it; stop = lowest low since the undercut) · **`ep_close_reclaim`** (never reaches the EP-day low, dips under the CLOSE, 5-min close back above; stop = the low of the dip) — **this is HIS idea, built 2026-08-30** · **`ep_high_break`** (no pullback, pushes through the EP-day high; stop = prior session low) · **`ep_close_620_prox`** (proximity fallback, 620 turn near the EP-day close). ▶ 🛑 **PASSIVE OBSERVER — THE LINE:** writes only `mi_delayed_entry_watch` / `mi_delayed_entry_trigger`, never trade state; imports nothing from `broker/`; **SILENT — no Telegram while evidence accrues** (his 2026-08-30 ruling: an unproven signal in his notifications becomes a de-facto trade signal). ▶ ⏳ **NOT JUDGEABLE UNTIL ~LATE SEPT, AND ITS CURRENT NUMBERS ARE A TRAP:** oldest fire 2026-08-25, **NINE sessions elapsed against a TWENTY-session window**. A stop settles the instant it is hit, so the 2,334 settled rows are **the losers by construction** and the 696 still-open rows are where any winner lives. That is why every `r_none_*` column reads exactly −1.00 with zero positives (that arm has no target — it can only end at its stop) and the trail arm reads −0.62 to −0.75. **`ep_close_reclaim` currently shows the worst trail average of the four (−0.75R, 8.5% positive, n=623) and quoting that as a verdict on his idea would be a false negative off half a population.** ▶ ⚠ **Exclude `stop_width_pct < 0.5` from every read** — `mfe_r` carries BCTX at 5499R, the #621/#623 near-zero-stop class (99 of 3,129 rows). ▶ **ETA 2026-09-22 = the first date the earliest fires complete 20 sessions and the lane can be read honestly.** DoD: a tail-rate read per pattern on settled 20-session outcomes, measured on the TAIL not the median (`analysis_standard.md` §THE STATISTIC), with the near-zero-stop rows excluded → then his call on whether any pattern graduates. Full working: `docs/analysis/519_chart_vision_eval_cost_2026-09-12.md`. ⚠ **THE FIRST ANSWER WAS $116 AND IT WAS WRONG — because it priced a corpus this line's own prose described six weeks ago.** The card could not reach prod, so it took n=227 from PLAN.md and said so. I ran its own query against prod: **the corpus is 372**, 64% larger. The per-alert cost was right; the population was stale. **A price read out of a task line is stale on arrival — re-run the count, never quote the prose.** ▶ **TWO FINDINGS WORTH KEEPING:** the harness prints its own $85 estimate assuming 250 output tokens per call, and the measured history (the judge's 500→1500 max_tokens raise, plus the #343 shadow's real spend) implies 450–715 — **so the tool understates its own cost by about a third**; and the harness recomputes the rate itself instead of importing `pricing_for()`, the same shape that mispriced a model by 50% for a day. ▶ **NOT A RECOMMENDATION TO SPEND: per the chain above, his labels come first** — this number exists so he can see what he is deferring, not to be acted on today. >> 📋 **THE STEPS, SO WHOEVER PICKS THIS UP DOES NOT RE-DERIVE THEM (operator 2026-09-12: *"include these details in the plan/tasks so when we pick it up we have clear steps"*).** ▶ **STEP 0 — HIS LABELS FIRST, and this is a gate not a preference.** #594's `chart_reading_review_cycle` surfaces the next sample; his rulings land in `tests/fixtures/must_not_trade_charts.py` keyed on (ticker, date) with his words verbatim, and `scripts/probes/_structure_read_v3.py` encodes them for **$0**. Nothing below is legitimate until the paid run has that bar to beat — otherwise a poor score reads as *vision does not work* when it means *we never told it what to look for*. ▶ **STEP 1 — A PILOT, AND ITS PURPOSE IS THE DELTA YIELD, NOT THE PLUMBING.** ⚠ The harness's own smoke mode (`--limit 4`) validates machinery and render rate and says so in its docstring — it **cannot** answer this. **The run's only output is the rows where the chart CHANGED the verdict; every unchanged row is spend with nothing to label.** The single prior read is **5 changes in 56 shadow rows (~9%)**. If that holds, the full run yields ~33 labelable charts; at 2% it yields 7, and $190 buys almost nothing. **Run ~60 alerts (~$16) and count the changes** — that separates 9% from 2% well enough to decide, and it also tells him how many charts he would be asked to look at, which is HIS time and not just money. ▶ **STEP 2 — DECIDE ON YIELD, not on hope.** Enough deltas → run the remainder (~372 alerts total, ~$190 whole path incl. one re-run, $0.26/alert). Too few → the eval cannot answer the question at any price, and that is itself the finding — do not buy the full run to confirm a thin yield. ▶ **STEP 3 — HE LABELS THE DELTAS (ADR 0011, the agent never self-scores), then the reintroduction decision is his.** ⚠ Do NOT resurrect the twice-daily cron; the pause stands until the offline read earns it back. ⚠ Do NOT put the SCORER choice to him again — `check_plan._standing_ask_gate` fails the commit if it reappears in the ask table. ▶ **BEFORE ANY SPEND, RE-COUNT.** The corpus was 227 six weeks ago and is 372 today; a price quoted from this line's prose is stale on arrival. Re-run the count query in `docs/analysis/519_chart_vision_eval_cost_2026-09-12.md` §Method and rescale at $0.26/alert. >> [blocked:2026-09-12 → 2026-09-24 — NOT a roll, a date that was impossible as written. ## Operational safety / hardening
- #677 | 2026-09-22 | deployed | 🔴 **A SOURCE GAP CAPS AN EP AT MODERATE AND MODERATE DOES NOT GET ENTERED — AND NOTHING CONNECTS THE TWO SURFACES.** Operator, 2026-09-20: *"we don't want source gaps to cause us to miss real EPs or even potential EPs, and if this becomes a larger gap"* — and he was right that something exists, but it is only half. ▶ **WHAT EXISTS:** the weekly source-gap finder (#235/#211) names ~one stock a week whose move we could not source. **WHAT DOES NOT:** any link from that to the missed-EP side. They are two sections of the SAME weekly review and nothing joins them. 🔴 **CIFR 2026-09-16 IS THE PROOF, and it is in both sections of the 09-20 edition:** the source-gap finder said we could not source its +9.2% move (ERCOT disclosed grid access for 3.2 GW across its Texas sites); the should've-entered table said *"MODERATE — not entered"* at **+16%**. The row itself reads `skip_category=moderate_tier`, `catalyst_quality=strong`. **A STRONG catalyst, capped at MODERATE, not entered — because we could not source it.** ▶ **THE MECHANISM IS THE JUDGE'S OWN WRITTEN RULE** (`ep_grade_judge.py:168`): *"If no fresh verifiable driver exists, say the driver is UNIDENTIFIED and grade only what is verifiable — MODERATE at best"*, and *"has_direct_source=false combined with a materiality-driven promotion is the highest-risk pattern: prefer the floor tier"*. That rule is CORRECT — we should not promote on an unverifiable story. The defect is that nobody measures what it costs. 📊 **SCALE, 60 days (prod, 2026-09-20):** `moderate_tier` **26 names, 18 of them with a strong or game_changer catalyst, avg peak +11%**; `catalyst_downgrade` 37 names, 0 strong, avg peak +14%. **The 18 are the population** — a weak catalyst capped is the rule working; a STRONG one capped is either a real source gap or a judge miss, and today we cannot tell which. ▶ **DoD: for every should've-entered name whose skip is tier- or catalyst-related, the weekly review states whether we HAD a direct source for it — so a source gap and a missed EP appear as ONE line, not two sections.** Then the standing question becomes answerable: of the strong-catalyst names capped at MODERATE, how many were capped for want of a source we could have had? VERIFY: the next edition renders at least one such name with its source status attached, and the count of strong-catalyst-capped names is reported with its denominator. WOULD-FAIL-IF: a name appears in the source-gap section and the missed-EP section of the same edition without either referencing the other. ⚖ **REPORTING ONLY — this does NOT change the judge, the tier rule or admission.** The rule that caps an unsourced catalyst is correct and stays; this measures its cost so a future ruling has a number. Any change to the grading path is CHANGE_PROCESS + sign-off (THE LINE). ⚠ **NOT the same as onboarding sources one at a time** — that was raised the same day and declined on evidence: six source gaps in five weeks came from five DIFFERENT source types (a grid regulator, an analyst note, a press wire, an IR page, one never found), so wiring any single one buys exactly one historical name. Revisit onboarding only when a source TYPE repeats. Refs #235, #211, #210, #662. [Operational safety / hardening] >> 🔧 **BUILT SAME DAY on his "why not fix now, stop always deferring" — card commit `ef4271bb` in an isolated worktree, NOT merged and NOT deployed.** Filing it had been the wrong call: none of the four allowed deferral reasons applied, it is a reporting change, and the generator it sits in was rebuilt hours earlier (#662). ⚠ **HOLDING MERGE ON ONE THING: the card's 9-sourced / 8-gap / 1-unassessable split is ILLUSTRATIVE, built from the denominator of 18 I had already measured — its worktree has no live DB. The real split must be run against prod before any number reaches him.** >> 🔴 **MEASURED AGAINST PROD 2026-09-20 — AND IT RETRACTS THIS LINE'S OWN HEADLINE EXAMPLE.** **CIFR reads `has_direct_source: TRUE`.** We DID have a direct source in its corpus at grade time (an SEC filing or a Benzinga wire), so its MODERATE cap was a **JUDGE CALL, not a source gap** — the opposite of what this line was filed on, and of what I told him twice. ▶ **HOW I GOT IT WRONG:** the two review sections ask DIFFERENT questions and I read them as one. The source-gap finder asks *can we explain this move from the feeds we scrape?* (CIFR: no — the news was at ERCOT). The judge asks *did this alert's corpus contain a direct source?* (CIFR: yes). Both are true and they are not the same question. 📊 **THE REAL SPLIT, 60 days: of 18 strong-catalyst names capped at MODERATE, only 3 were UNSOURCED. 15 HAD a direct source and were capped anyway. 0 unassessable.** And across the top 40 should've-entered rows, exactly ONE is a source gap: **BLZE 2026-07-31, peak +78%**. 🔭 **SO THE FINDING INVERTS, and this is the useful part: source gaps are a SMALL cause (3 of 18). The open question is the FIFTEEN names that had a direct source, carried a strong or game-changer catalyst, and were still capped at MODERATE.** That is a judge-calibration question, not a sourcing one, and it is where the +11% average peak actually sits. ⚖ Not a change to make — the capping rule is correct and stays; this is what to look at next. ⚠ **The card's own 9/8/1 split was ILLUSTRATIVE and it said so** (its worktree had no live DB). Merging on it would have shipped a false story to him; the numbers above are the measured ones. **EXPECT:** next Sunday's edition renders every tier-/catalyst-skipped row with a SRC/GAP marker and the summary line carries 3/18 with its window. UNINTENDED: a row marked GAP that actually had a source (the has_markers exclusion mis-firing), or the summary counting the unassessable as unsourced. 🎯 **DONE-WHEN:** one edition renders the marker and the 3-of-18 summary. **VERIFY-LIVE =** that message. WOULD-FAIL-IF: a name appears in both sections of one edition with neither referencing the other. >> ✅ **THE INVESTIGATIVE QUESTION IS ANSWERED AND IT COMES OUT BENIGN — measured on prod 2026-09-20.** His worry was *"we don't want source gaps to cause us to miss real EPs"*. Measured: **they don't.** 📊 **Forward outcomes of the 18 strong-catalyst names capped at MODERATE, split by whether we had a source:** the **15 that HAD a source** averaged peak +11.0% and **−3.1% at five days**, with 1 of 15 peaking ≥20% and 7 of 14 closing positive. The **3 REAL source gaps** (ALAB, ERO, SLN) averaged peak +8.2% and **−12.0% at five days, with ZERO of 3 closing positive.** 🎯 **SO THE CAP IS WORKING, on both arms.** The judge was right to cap the 15 — they went nowhere. And the three we could not source were the WORST of the cohort: source gaps are filtering LOSERS, not costing winners. ⛔ **THEREFORE: no admission work follows from this, and that is the finding.** It is the third admission thread this week to measure benign after a peak-shaped number looked alarming (the cooldown, the extension cap, this). The standing conclusion on #482 holds unchanged — the problem is CAPTURE, not what we let in. ⚠ **Carry the same limit as the others:** these are hold-to-a-date measures, not what the live bracket would have returned. They are enough to say nothing is being missed; they are not an R figure. ▶ **The instrument SHIPS AND STAYS** — the SRC/GAP marker and the 3-of-18 denominator are what made this checkable in an hour instead of being argued about for a month, and they will catch it if the ratio moves.

At 08:15:07 ET the news path took a 429, waited 6.4s and succeeded; 1.3s later the catalyst path took the same 429 and hard-failed and paged. The shared rule is now `collector.pplx_429_wait_s`. This also makes the ALERT honest: `llm_health` calls 429 actionable on the ground *"429-after-retries"*, which was false here. ▶ **(3) THE ALERT GUESSED OVER AN ANSWER IT ALREADY HAD.** It told him to *"check our API key/plan"* for a 429 whose captured body read `{"message":"upstream model is overloaded","type":"overloaded"}`. It now quotes the provider. And the shared consequence sentence claimed the **RS universe** was degrading — Perplexity does not feed it; consequences are per-provider now. ▶ ⚠ **NOT CLAIMED, because the adversarial pass caught the overclaim:** a zero news score is NOT a marker of an outage — `_is_garbage()` returns the byte-identical `(0, "", False)` from a SUCCESSFUL "no news" answer, and **2026-07-21 is a zero-news theme row on a day with no provider failure at all**. This fix removes that ambiguity GOING FORWARD; it does not reinterpret history, and no historical theme score is restated. ▶ ⚖ **CLASSIFIED AS A BUG FIX, NOT A CRITERIA CHANGE, UNDER HIS OWN 2026-08-27 RULING** — *"make sure any issue with perplexity doesn't affect live trades, just render as no-op or unavailable input."* A timeout demoting a theme and stripping its tickers' EP bonus is the OPPOSITE of a no-op, so this enforces a rule he already signed rather than proposing a new one; it restores the neutral-15 behaviour the code already documents and intends, and no threshold, entry, exit, sizing or safeguard is touched. SSoT updated in the same change (`docs/architecture/theme_engine.md` §"How a NEWS-PROVIDER failure scores a theme"). Call sites DERIVED by AST, not listed: exactly two (`_rescore_existing_theme` 3825, `_score_new_theme` 6824). [[classify-before-applying-evidence-gates]] ▶ 5 mutations run RED (remove the retry · retry everything · re-swallow the failure · raise for every caller · drop the body extraction). Suite 8674. ▶ **EXPECT:** against a baseline where `theme_news_api_err` has never once been true, the NEXT Perplexity failure during a nightly theme run writes `news_score=15` and KEEPS the prior description instead of blanking it, and produces NO `theme_low_quality_description` cap for that theme. On a clean night nothing changes at all — no theme's score, stage or description differs from what today's code would produce. UNINTENDED, watched together: a theme holding a stale description for days because failures repeat (the 15 is deliberately half credit, so score still decays), or a 429 storm now costing ~2x the wall-clock on the catalyst path during an EP scan. **DONE-WHEN:** one provider failure lands during a theme run and the theme keeps its stage, AND one clean nightly run shows no diff vs the prior night's stage distribution. **VERIFY:** `mi_audit_log` shows an `api_failure_perplexity` during a nightly theme window with NO `theme_low_quality_description` row within 100ms of it. **WOULD-FAIL-IF:** a `theme_low_quality_description` cap still lands inside 100ms of a Perplexity failure row, or a clean night's stage distribution shifts.

The Lane-2 silence this line was filed for is a SYMPTOM, not the defect** — I filed the one missing audit row I had looked at, and the real population was 23 jobs. The same error as the deal-pin pools and the #667 consumers, three days running: **the arithmetic was right, the population was wrong.** [[derive-the-population-never-hand-list-it]] ▶ **WHAT IS MEASURED, from `mi_job_runs` (not inferred):** `nightly_data_pull` started 21:00 UTC and ran **26,192 s — 7.3 HOURS — against a 740–1,070 s norm over the prior eight weekdays**, and ended `interrupted` (*"cancelled — process shutdown/restart mid-run"*) when MY OWN #664 deploy restarted the container at ~04:16 UTC. **In the 21:13–22:10 UTC window every weekday from 09-10 to 09-17 ran 22–23 jobs. Friday ran ZERO.** The 23 missed by name: alert_rank_shadow, consolidation_readiness, coverage_probe, crypto_nightly_ingest, delayed_entry_shadow, drift_check, **evening_briefing**, exit_path_shadow, flag_continuation_scan, htf_management_shadow, judge_eval_divergence_check, live_fill_counterfactuals, model_resolution_refresh, parabolic_scan, post_nightly_audit, sell_discipline_recorder, spend_alarm, strength_spread_alert, sugar_babies_cohort_refresh, theme_axis_co_move_refresh, theme_axis_eod_unscored, theme_synthesis, wick_forward_returns. ▶ **The chain RECOVERED at 23:00 UTC** (`coverage_watch_late` ran, and it is NOT execution-owned — so the container was not dead, and my first hypothesis, a frozen event loop for the full 7.3 h, is WRONG and is recorded here so nobody re-derives it). Every missed job's `misfire_grace_time` is 900–3,600 s, so a ~1.5–2 h stall is sufficient to skip the whole window by misfire. ▶ **NOT A ONE-OFF:** `nightly_data_pull` carries **4 `interrupted` runs since 2026-08-10 and 18 `aborted`** against 56 successes. ⚠ **Friday's container logs are GONE** — the deploy recreated the container, so `docker logs` returns nothing for that window. `mi_job_runs` is the only surviving witness, which is itself a finding about what we can diagnose after a deploy. ✅ **ROOT CAUSE, found 2026-09-19 and evidenced in code — THE JOB HAS NO TIMEOUT ANYWHERE, AND NEITHER DOES THE DB POOL.** `_nightly_data_pull` (scheduler.py:337-882, ~30k chars) contains **ZERO `asyncio.wait_for` and ZERO `timeout=`**, and `asyncpg.create_pool` (db.py:154) is `min_size=1, max_size=5` with **no `command_timeout`** — only two query sites in that whole module pass one. So a hang anywhere in the job is UNBOUNDED and holds until the process dies, which is exactly what happened. **Same class as #664's alpaca finding: no timeout means the caller never comes back.** ▶ **WHERE it hung, bounded by evidence:** the job's last audit event is `theme_constituent_churn` at **21:13:01** (emitted at `theme_engine.py:8748`, the END of the theme block); the next event from it is the 04:16:32 cancel. So it died in **step 8 (`detect_state_changes` / `send_state_alerts`), step 9 (`settle_open_shadows`), or `_check_nightly_silent_errors`** — each wrapped in try/except, so an EXCEPTION would not have hung it. - #666 | 2026-09-22 | deployed | 🔴 **THE CATALYST FACT-CHECK MONITOR ACCUSES ON A CORRELATION WHEN IT COULD COUNT THE ACTUAL CASES — it tripped tonight at p=0.086 and printed revert SQL for a live grading change.** ▶ **WHERE THIS CAME FROM** (operator 2026-09-15, after the alert): *"Don't revert, keep monitoring but make monitor more conclusive, is the fact checking good or bad? Help us decide."* ▶ **WHAT TRIPPED IT:** 2 HIGH alerts from 48 gapping stocks (4.2 per 100) against 31 of 269 (11.5 per 100) — a 63.8% fall past its 50% bar. **But at the prior rate 48 stocks yield 5.5 expected alerts and we saw 2: P(<=2) = 0.086, i.e. a coin does this 1 time in 12.** At 1 alert it would have been real (p=0.026). **It has now false-fired three times** (08-21, 08-24, 08-25 per its own header) and been hardened twice — era-scoped 08-24, supply-normalised 08-26. Both fixes improved the DENOMINATOR; neither gave it a way to be conclusive. ▶ 🔑 **THE FIX, AND THE MONITOR ALREADY HOLDS THE DATA: STOP CORRELATING, START COUNTING.** `mi_catalyst_tier_shadow` stamps the AI's grade and the fact-checked grade per ticker-day, and `mi_ep_scan_log.score_breakdown` carries the catalyst points that acted. A grade step is worth a KNOWN 12.5 presented points (25 vs 15 raw, through the 1.25x+15 rescale), so for every name the fact-check moved we can say exactly whether the move crossed the bar. **Measured by hand 2026-09-15 over all 109 rows since 2026-08-24: the fact-check changed 6 grades and PREVENTED EXACTLY 2 ALERTS — FEIM (58.8, would have been 71.3) and SMMT (55.0, would have been 67.5) — and CAUSED 0.** AGX alerted either way at 65; CHRN would have alerted anyway at 92.5 without its promotion; NBBK and ATEC stayed below the bar with or without. ⚠ **SCOPED TO THE MONITOR'S OWN RECENT WINDOW — which is what the shortfall is measured in — the fact-check explains 1 of the 3.5, not 2.** `_LATTICE_RECENT_DAYS = 7` calendar days ending on the run day, so tonight that is the trading days **2026-09-09..09-15**: **FEIM (09-11) is inside it; SMMT (09-03) is NOT** — SMMT sits in the 30-day PRIOR window, where preventing it LOWERED the very baseline the recent rate is judged against. Counting an out-of-window prevention against an in-window shortfall is the wrong-population error this whole task exists to stop, so the fix must window-scope the count to the monitor's own bucket, not to the era. 1 of 3.5 is ~30% — and the whole shortfall is inside noise anyway (p=0.086). ▶ **DoD: the nightly message states the number of alerts the fact-check actually prevented in the window and NAMES them, and it withholds the revert SQL when that count cannot account for the shortfall it is reporting.** WOULD-FAIL-IF: it still prints revert SQL on a rate comparison alone, or reports a prevented-count it cannot name the tickers for. ⚖ **This changes what the monitor MEASURES AND SAYS — never the 50% threshold, never the flip, never any grading rule** (the same line its 08-24 and 08-26 fixes drew). ▶ **AND IT ANSWERS HIS QUESTION AS IT ACCUMULATES:** of the 2 prevented, FEIM went **-0.5%** over 12 days (a dud, good save) and SMMT reached **+9.9%** (a real miss). 1-for-1 on two decisions is not a verdict — but a causal counter accrues toward one, where a rate comparison never can. ⚠ Weekend slot: it edits `health_checks.py`, market-agent owned. [b0] Board 71 -> 72 on his carryover. Refs #533, `catalyst_tier_shadow.py`, `health_checks.run_catalyst_lattice_monitor`, `docs/setups/magna53_ep.md` 2026-08-22. FEIM (`gc_demoted_scheduled_no_content_delta`) had `expct_beat=true` but again `expct_looking='unknown'`. ⚠ **This is not a bug — the lattice header states the asymmetry deliberately: the top tier is a positive claim, so unverifiable forward content keeps a name at `strong`.** But measured, the practical effect is different from the intent: **of the 9 names the AI graded `game_changer` since 08-22, the forward-guidance read was `forward` on 4, `unknown` on 4 and `mixed_fwd` on 1 — so on ~44% of the cases that matter the fact-check is deciding on information it does not have.** ▶ **THE REFRAMED QUESTION, which is his: is this a FACT-CHECK or a COVERAGE TEST?** Right now the extractor's ability to find forward guidance, not the news itself, decides the top grade almost half the time. **Fix the forward-guidance extraction and it becomes a fact-check; It was the #540 failure again: I quoted the DONE-WHEN (*"three alerts land as HTML… zero plain-text retries behind them"*), which is MET, while the DoD is broader and is NOT.** The DoD says *"the alerts that carry machine text — SQL, JSON, identifiers — are sent WITHOUT a Markdown parse mode… and `telegram_markdown_fallback` drops to zero for those senders"*. **"Those senders" is every alert carrying machine text, not the thirteen the migration commit happened to touch.** ▶ **THE ONE THAT IS LEFT, found by today's verify sweep and measured:** the 10:00 ET *"Real-time EP misses"* digest has 400'd and fallen back **4 of 4 days since the deploy** (09-14, 09-15, 09-16, 09-17, one row each at exactly 10:00:00 ET), and its payload carries `ep_rt_sustain_reject` and `ep_rt_halt_suspect` — machine text, squarely inside the DoD. 🔑 `ep_detector.py:3068` sends `send_telegram_message(" ".join(parts))` with **no `parse_mode`**; `git show --stat 144666a9` confirms the migration never touched that file. ▶ **REMAINING WORK = the same one-line migration, applied a fourteenth time, plus a test in the shape of `tests/test_647_machine_text_alerts_on_html_layer.py`.** Kept HERE rather than split to a new task: the DoD already covers it, and splitting would have been a burndown that closed nothing. ⚠ **What IS established and does not need re-checking:** 7 real firings of the migrated senders since 09-12 with zero fallbacks against them (pre-fix baseline 30 failures in 10 days), and payload integrity confirmed positively inside the RUNNING prod container — the real 09-14 string round-trips byte-identical and a synthetic worst case (`*bold*`, `&`, `<`, underscored identifiers) escapes correctly with every identifier unmangled. >> 🔧 **FIX BUILT 2026-09-17, AWAITING THE 12:00–13:00 ET DEPLOY (`market-agent`).** `ep_detector.send_rt_miss_digest` now sends `md_to_html(" ".join(parts))` with `parse_mode="HTML"` — the identical one-line change the other thirteen got, applied a fourteenth time. Test coverage added to `tests/test_647_machine_text_alerts_on_html_layer.py` (30 → 33): the sender joins the pinned source list, the REAL failing payload from the 09-14..17 prod rows is asserted to keep `ep_rt_sustain_reject` / `ep_rt_halt_suspect` through conversion with no stray `<i>`, and a bare-send guard. **RED-PROVEN: reverting the one line fails 2 of 33.** ⚖ Delivery only — no detection criterion, no threshold, no trade state, wording unchanged. ▶ **AFTER DEPLOY this closes on the DoD's own words when `telegram_markdown_fallback` reads zero for this sender across three consecutive firing days (the digest fires daily at 10:00 ET, so ~09-22), with the digest confirmed still ARRIVING** — a silent digest would read as zero fallbacks and look like success, which is the failure mode to watch. 🔒 **THE VERIFY, WITH ITS BLIND SPOT NAMED — `send_rt_miss_digest` WRITES NO AUDIT ROW OF ITS OWN.** It only READS `ep_rt_live_miss` / `ep_rt_universe_catch` / `ep_rt_declined_not_missed`, so there is no positive record that a digest was sent. **POSITIVE CONTROL instead of a bare absence:** on any day those source rows exist, the digest HAD content and must have sent — so the check is *source rows present AND zero `telegram_markdown_fallback` AND zero `telegram_send_failed`*, not merely 'no fallback'. ⚠ **RESIDUAL, stated because it is exactly this repo's recurring trap:** the send sits inside `except Exception: pass`, so a failure in `md_to_html` or the import itself would produce NO row of any kind and read identically to success. Broker-confirmed at 16:50: HOOD and SEI hold stops, OKTA holds 2 shares and none. 🔴 **THE MITIGATION GAP, which is the actual task:** every repair path closes BEFORE the cycle that can strip coverage runs. `_stop_coverage_repair_retry_job` is windowed **09:31-15:55** and returns early outside it · `sync_positions`/`eod_cleanup` is **16:05** · `naked_position_post_refresh_check` ran **16:27** and was correctly green, because the stop still existed then · **the stop-replacement cycle runs at 16:45, after all of them.** So a stop lost after ~16:30 is unprotected until the next session's 09:31 — **on a Friday that is ~64 hours.** ⛔ **AND THE ALERT LIED BY OMISSION: it told him *"Remediation runs at 4:05 PM ET — monitor"* at 16:45, when 16:05 had already passed.** A message that promises a repair which cannot come is worse than silence — he stood down on it. ⛔ **A SECOND HOLE: the 16:02 exit rejection wrote NO audit row.** The only record is the Telegram. Same class as #184's four unexplained June cancels. ▶ **DoD: (a) coverage is re-checked AFTER the last event that can remove it, not before — the check must outlive the replacement cycle; (b) a stop lost outside market hours is repaired or escalated, never left to the next session; (c) the unprotected alert states the NEXT ACTUAL repair time, computed, never a hardcoded hour; (d) a rejected exit writes an audit row.** WOULD-FAIL-IF: a stop cancelled at 16:45 still shows no follow-up check before the next open. ⚖ **Detection, timing and reporting — NOT the stop rule, the exit rule or sizing. Any change to what the stop IS stays his.** Refs #596, #523/#151, #599, #184. [Operational safety / hardening] >> 🔴 **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-09-11 — it is not a scheduling gap, it is `execute_full_exit` itself (`broker/order_manager.py:~4728`).** The function does, in this order: **(1) cancel the stop order · (2) `alpaca.close_position(...)` · (3) on any exception, Telegram *"Full exit FAILED"* and `return False`.** **There is no rollback and no stop replacement on the failure path — the position is left naked BY CONSTRUCTION every time step 2 fails.** ⛔ **AND STEP 1 IS WHAT MAKES STEP 2 FAIL.** The rejection was *insufficient qty available (requested 2, available 0, held_for_orders 2)* — Alpaca had not yet released the shares the just-cancelled stop was holding. **The cancel and the sell race each other, and the function causes its own failure.** ▶ **SEVERITY: this is not confined to after hours.** Any full exit — including a real stop-driven exit mid-session — can lose its stop and keep its shares while price is moving. No audit row records the attempt. ▶ **DoD (a) supersedes the earlier wording: `execute_full_exit` must not leave a position unprotected — either sell first and cancel on fill, or restore the stop on every failure path before returning.** ⚖ Order-of-operations and failure handling only — the stop PRICE, the exit rule and sizing are untouched and stay his. >> 🔨 **THE CRASH HALF IS BUILT AND PUSHED 2026-09-11 (`7340072e`), NOT DEPLOYED.** `execute_full_exit` now (1) waits for the broker to release the shares after cancelling — `_await_shares_released` polls `qty_available` (#151), bounded ~5s, and is an OPTIMISATION not a gate, so a broker reporting oddly can never block an exit; and (2) **re-places the stop at its own price on ANY sell failure before returning** — `_restore_stop_after_failed_exit` — with the Telegram now saying which of the two states he is in. 6 tests, both halves RED-proven by reverting each in turn. ⚠ **One of my own assertions was itself weak and I caught it only on the RED run: `"RESTORED" in msg` is a substring of `"STOP NOT RESTORED"`, so it passed in BOTH states — a test that cannot fail, on the day's own theme. - #597 | 2026-09-30 | deployed | 🐛 **If a position vanishes from the broker mid-flight, we close the row with no exit leg and book the wrong P&L.** Found 2026-08-25 by the deployed-broker review. `sync_positions`' "position gone from Alpaca" branch (`order_manager.py:5805-5816`) sets `remaining_shares=0` and closes the trade **without recording an exit leg**. It can race a #591 stays-open row: the resting exit fills at the broker, our websocket finaliser has not caught up, `sync_positions` polls in that gap, and the row closes with the fill unrecorded. **The booked P&L is then wrong and no exit leg explains it.** ✅ It is now DETECTED rather than silent — the #588 L1 invariant `exit_share_sum_mismatch` fires on exactly this shape, and its docstring's claim that the state "can no longer exist" is true only for the paths #591 touched, not for this one. ▶ **DoD: make that branch record the exit it implies (or refuse to close and let the finaliser do it), so the invariant stops being the only thing standing between a race and a wrong number.** ⚠ Detection is not correction: by the time the L1 fires, the trade's P&L is already wrong and feeds `mi_sell_discipline_records`, which every exit-rule replay reads. ⚖ **Money path — `broker/` runs on apollo-execution, needs `deploy.sh execution` as a second step.** Recording bug, not a discipline change, but a change to when a row closes is his call. Same file and class as **#596** (a partly-filled leg cancelled with no replacement) — worth doing together. [Operational safety / hardening] ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-29 (Fable card, verified inline before acceptance).** New `order_manager._resolve_position_gone`, a HYBRID of the DoD's two options because each alone re-creates a known failure: blind-close books $0 on a real loss (the bug), and refuse-only leaves a phantom open row whenever the WS event is genuinely LOST. Under the #151 per-trade advisory try-lock, re-reading the row with the `account_mode` filter: **(1) GRACE** — stop confirmed filled <10 min ago → defer, the finaliser owns it; **(2) RECORD** — older fill → book the exit from BROKER TRUTH ONLY (`filled_avg_price`/`filled_qty`/`filled_at`), exits + `total_pnl` + `remaining_shares` in ONE statement (the MNDY 2026-05-11 invariant), idempotent on `exits[].order_id`; **(3) REFUSE** — no confirmable fill → **row stays OPEN and LOUD** (audit + Telegram + a discrepancy line every sweep). ⚠ **NEVER SYNTHESISES A PRICE** — a wrong-but-confident P&L is the exact failure being fixed. ⚖ **CLOSE-TIMING CHANGED and it is declared in the code comment** (was: unconditional same-sweep close; now: same-sweep only on a confirmed fill) — recording only, no order placed/cancelled/altered, revert = delete the helper and restore the blind UPDATE. 9 tests, mutation-verified by the card (pre-fix 9/9 red; exit-leg removed, idempotency no-op'd, grace zeroed, total_pnl decoupled — all red) **and independently by me: mutating it to accept an unconfirmed fill turned 2 tests red.** ⚠ **A FOURTH GATE — `audit_column_writes` (trade-state ownership, the BW/KLAR/CRMD class) — REJECTED THE FIRST CUT with exit 6, and it was right:** the helper wrote `exits` / `remaining_shares` / `total_pnl` / `closed_at` / `status` itself, which would have made a SIXTH writer of trade-state columns. The registry's own history records that T1.3 on 2026-05-18 REMOVED a duplicate writer for exactly this reason (*"close-path delegated to finalize_stop_fill which is the canonical writer"*), so the gate's option (a) — register the new writer — would have undone that consolidation. **Took option (b):** case 2's commit now delegates to `_finalize_stop_fill_locked`, and the card's own idempotency guard + Telegram were DELETED as redundant rather than left as a second copy (the canonical writer owns both, and also nulls `stop_order_id`). ✅ **The deadlock risk I flagged was checked, not assumed:** the lock is NOT re-entrant cross-connection, so the public wrapper would self-deadlock — it calls the `_locked` variant while holding the try-lock. **I verified independently that the `_locked` variant never acquires** (zero lock calls in its body). Gate now exit 0. Suite 6534. ▶ **VERIFY-LIVE = the next `sync_positions` sweep logs no `sync_position_gone_unresolved` for a healthy book, and any real occurrence Telegrams instead of silently closing.** Needs `deploy.sh execution` (broker/ runs there). UNINTENDED, watched together: any fallback row whose detail carries `mode=HTML(default)` is a converter defect on a real body (read its `md_body`); a message arriving with a literal `<b>` or `&lt;` is a caller that passed `"HTML"` for a non-HTML body. **DONE-WHEN:** 5 market days with (a) observed at least once on a default sender AND zero `mode=HTML(default)` fallback rows. **WOULD-FAIL-IF:** a `telegram_markdown_fallback` row cites an HTML tag error (`Unsupported start tag` / `Unmatched end tag` / `Can't find end tag`), or the operator sees a literal `<b>`. ⚠ NOT covered by this default and left as found: `edit_telegram_message` (own `"Markdown"` default, one caller), and the three separate httpx senders on legacy Markdown — `core/notifications.notify_owner`, `broker/telegram_confirm.send_trade_proposal`, `channels/telegram._send_chunk`. VERIFY-LIVE = the first DEFAULT-path digest carrying a fenced code block arrives FORMATTED on its first send, with the block opening on its first line and identifiers such as `stop_order_id` intact — and `mi_audit_log` holds ZERO `telegram_markdown_fallback` rows whose detail reads `mode=HTML(default)`. WOULD-FAIL-IF: a fallback row appears with that mode (the flip is 400-ing and silently retrying as plain text), or a surface already on HTML starts arriving double-escaped. 📋 EXPECT: against today's baseline of 0 prod 400s across the 29 already-HTML surfaces and 266 corpus bodies that FAIL today, the Monday digests should render where they previously broke, the 29 should be untouched, and the fallback count should stay at zero. UNINTENDED to watch in the same breath: a long message arriving with a split tag, or a body that was already HTML coming through escaped. 🎯 DONE-WHEN: three consecutive weekdays of digests with zero `mode=HTML(default)` fallback rows and no report of a mangled message. >> ✅ **FLIPPED, DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING IMAGE 2026-09-19.** Deployed `both` then `execution` (weekend, unrestricted). **Acceptance case run inside `apollo-market` with the transport stubbed and the payload inspected — nothing was sent:** an UNMODIFIED default sender, given a bolded line plus a fenced `SELECT * FROM mi_live_trades WHERE stop_order_id IS NULL`, posts **`parse_mode=HTML`**, the SQL **verbatim with `stop_order_id` intact**, and the code block **opening on its first line** — the blank-line defect that was live on the L1/L2 pages is gone. The default now reads `<parse_mode not passed: convert Markdown→HTML>`. ▶ **Built in the order the line demanded, because the order WAS the deliverable:** fence patch → sentinel (32 of 44 explicit-HTML bodies would otherwise double-convert) → tag-aware chunker → the flip. ▶ **The risk set the 732-body corpus could NOT vouch for was addressed head-on:** all 29 already-HTML surfaces pass `parse_mode` explicitly so they take the passthrough branch; a body under 4,000 chars is the identity through the new chunker; old-vs-new conversion over 641 bodies is **612 identical and 29 differing ONLY by the removed newline, 0 differing any other way**. The render check re-run after the patch moves *real bodies where HTML is worse* from **19 → 0** and holds HTML-400s at **0 of 732**. 21 RED-proved assertions, 0 new source pins, suite 8559. ⚠ **Found and NOT fixed, outside this DoD:** `edit_telegram_message` keeps its own `Markdown` default (one caller), and three separate httpx senders remain on legacy Markdown — `core/notifications.notify_owner`, `broker/telegram_confirm.send_trade_proposal` and `channels/telegram._send_chunk`. 🔴 **FIRST READ IS SUNDAY 2026-09-20 08:00 ET, NOT MONDAY — read it before anything else that morning** (advisor review, 09-19 evening). `weekly_system_review` (`scheduler.py:6928`, `CronTrigger(day_of_week="sun", hour=8)`) is the ONE known body that clears 4,000 chars, so it is the first and only thing that exercises `chunk_html`'s tag-closing seam on a real message; the 732-body corpus could not test a split. ⚠ It sends with an EXPLICIT `parse_mode="HTML"` (`system_review.py:299`), so it is a PASSTHROUGH, not a default-sender conversion — the seam is the chunker, and the chunker runs for explicit-HTML callers too (`briefing.py:2704`). WOULD-FAIL-IF: the review arrives in two parts and either half shows a literal `<b>`/`<pre>`, an unclosed bold or code block, or a `telegram_markdown_fallback` row cites `Unmatched end tag`/`Can't find end tag`. So waiting for a natural split is a bad verification plan; it would leave the seam unproven for months. ⚠ **The chunk count is recorded NOWHERE** — not in logs, `mi_job_runs` or `mi_audit_log` — so this could not be settled by reading, only by measuring. 🔒 **SO THE SEAM WAS VERIFIED DIRECTLY INSTEAD, and this is stronger than the one-message test it replaces: every one of the 641 real corpus bodies was chunked at five limits (4000/2000/1000/500/300) — 3,205 calls, 640 of which really split — and EVERY chunk is tag-balanced across all nine Telegram tags (`b i u s code pre a tg-spoiler blockquote`). ZERO unbalanced chunks.** ⚠ Stated limit, because it is a different claim from last night's: the 09-19 check proved old-vs-new `chunk_html` are byte-identical (the refactor is safe); THIS one proves the output is well-formed (the chunker is correct). ## Theme ecosystems — primary → sub-theme hierarchy (ADR 0032)
- #644 | 2026-11-14 | deployed | **Build DOWN-DAY RESILIENCE — the one leg of subtle RS that has never existed — and test it against the thing that beat three recommendations today.** 🔎 **WHY IT SURFACED (2026-09-11, #639):** the board's weekly churn is not renaming (1 in 152) and is not explained by basket size. **Board membership is near-MEMORYLESS: given a cohort has held N consecutive weeks, the chance it holds the next is 52 / 55 / 62 / 50 / 58 percent — flat at every history length.** So presence in an `rs_avg`-ranked top 30 is not an actionable signal, and three recommendations that tried to fix the BOARD (a size floor, a two-week confirmation, a weeks-held label) each failed on the data. `docs/analysis/639_board_churn_split_2026-09-11.md`. 🎯 **THE NORTH STAR SAYS WHAT TO MEASURE INSTEAD** — his own words: *"may not have highest RS but rising, rising faster, and/or holding up better in market down days."* `get_rs_velocity` (db.py:9411) and `get_rs_turners` (db.py:9779) cover the first two. **Down-day resilience does not exist anywhere in `agents/` or `shared/` — checked 2026-09-11 — and it is the UNCONTAMINATED one:** a gap manufactures an RS level, but it cannot manufacture *held up better on the market's down days over the previous month*, which is measured BEFORE the gap exists [[ep-edge-is-surprise-not-magnitude]]. ▶ **BUILD ($0, `mi_daily_closes`, 13.6M rows already on disk):** over the 20 sessions before a date, on the days SPY fell, the name's median return minus SPY's. Positive = it held up better. Read-only; no scoring, sizing or admission path touched. ▶ **DoD — the measure is NOT the deliverable, the TEST is:** compute resilience as-of each cohort's first board week, then ask whether a week-1 cohort with positive resilience holds its slot at a rate materially above the **55% base rate**. - #540 | 2026-09-30 | deployed | 🔴 **A live entry was rejected on a stock that ran +33%, and Alpaca had TOLD us why — we were discarding it on every rejection.** INSM 2026-08-06: buy 7 @ stop_limit 129.41/130.06 with an OTO stop at 126.15, submitted 13:31:11.892606Z, `failed_at` 13:31:11.896Z — **3.4 MILLISECONDS**, i.e. pre-trade validation, not a market event. The stock was +32.6% by 09:35 and +33.5% by 09:40. **ALPACA'S OWN WORDS, recovered:** `"reason": "[6098] Stop Price Already Triggered/Exceeds $ Threshold"`. **WHERE IT HID:** not on the order object (checked every key of the raw REST payload) — it is on the trade-updates EVENT beside the order, and the SDK's `TradeUpdate` model has NO `reason` field, so `raw_data=False` parses it away before our handler runs. Every rejection we have ever had arrived with a reason we threw out. ⚠ **Hours were spent inferring** — ruling out buying power ($17,355 vs a $910 order), account state, symbol restrictions, an in-the-money trigger, and a paper probe (`scripts/probes/_540_oto_rejection_probe.py`, 6 cases) that DID find a real rule (`stop_loss.stop_price must be <= base_price - 0.01`, base_price = the LIMIT price) which **does not explain INSM** (126.15 ≤ 130.05 passes). ⚠ **An external research pass proposed 'market price was below the stop-loss' — FALSIFIED by our own data**: last was $128.96, above the $126.15 stop. **SHIPPED:** `alpaca_client.fetch_broker_reject_reason()` — a targeted lookup against the trade-events history, called ONLY on reject/cancel/expire, never on the fill path; the reason lands in the audit detail as `broker_reason` AND in the operator Telegram. Deliberately NOT `raw_data=True`: that would rewrite every handler on the FILL path to work on dicts to recover a field needed only on a terminal failure. Never raises; a missing reason degrades silently. 7 tests. Proven against the real INSM order: returns the 6098 line. ▶ **VERIFY 8/07: the next broker rejection carries `broker_reason` in its audit row and its Telegram.** >> 🔴 **VERIFY 2026-08-07 FAILED — the capture returned NULL on the first real rejection since it shipped.** QNST was cancelled by the venue at 09:31:10 and the audit detail recorded **`"broker_reason": null`** — yet the reason was sitting right there on the event stream (`"Unsolicited: Bad Stop 19.8"`), and I retrieved it BY HAND from the same endpoint minutes later. So the mechanism is wired and the lookup does not find what a manual query finds. ⚠ **Do not close this on the INSM test** — that proved the function can read a `rejected` event after the fact; this is a `canceled` event, read live, and it came back empty. Candidate causes, none confirmed: the 4-second window is too tight for a 6 ms cancel whose event may not be queryable yet; or the handler filters on `rejected` and a `canceled` event never reaches the lookup. **DoD: the next broker cancel OR rejection carries a non-null `broker_reason` in its audit row and Telegram — proven on a real live event, not a replay.** This is the exact defect class the task exists to close: the broker told us why, twice, and both times we threw it away. - #312 | 2026-11-15 | pending | P5 Capital & Autonomy Ladder (H2→H3): unified_allocator live + formal shadow→paper→live ladder w/ auto-DEMOTION on breach + scaling/slippage telemetry >> ⚠ RED-TEAM PRE-FLIP GATE (docs/analysis/composition_redteam_2026-07-12.md RED-3, code-verified): the composite sizing multiplier is applied AFTER the 20%-capital/1%-risk caps (entry_pipeline.py:418) with NO upper re-cap — the moment the allocator writes a >1.0 multiplier it sizes STRAIGHT THROUGH every safeguard. Latent today (all multipliers ≤1.0). BEFORE the allocator can write >1.0 live: bound the composite multiplier OR re-cap position_size/risk after it. Non-negotiable safeguard fix — must land before this flip. >> **CLAMP SHIPPED 7/13 (rulings-pack R5, operator-ruled)**: `_apply_composite_multiplier` (pure, 5 tests) — up-never-past-builder-baseline (re-imposes 20%/VIX-risk caps with zero duplicated math), emits `sizing_multiplier_clamped`; deployed BOTH money containers, ≤1.0 ramps byte-identical. RED-3 pre-flip gate SATISFIED. [b2] [ok:2026-09-01→2026-11-15 — OPERATOR-DIRECTED PRIORITY RESET 2026-08-22 ("reset the board based on priority"): dates now express TIER, not accumulated drift. T5.] >> 🎯 **DoD (written 2026-09-10): the shadow→paper→live ladder is CODE with automatic demotion on breach — not a documented convention — proven by a forced breach demoting a strategy in a test. WOULD-FAIL-IF: demotion requires a human to notice, which is the failure mode a ladder exists to remove. ⚖ THE LINE: any live rung needs operator sign-off.** ⚠ This is a ROADMAP CONTAINER — its bar is that the named work lands or is ruled out, not that a single artifact appears. >> 🔴 **SECOND PRE-FLIP GATE, FOUND 2026-09-14 — THE PHASE-1A TELEMETRY CANNOT VALIDATE THE FLIP, BECAUSE THE ALLOCATOR RUNS AT 9:35 ET AND ORB ENTRIES SUBMIT AT 9:31.** `docs/analysis/unified_allocator_phase_1b_2026-09-14.md`. `cross_strategy_allocator.py:252` reads `get_open_position_count()` LIVE at 9:35, so `slots_available` already excludes that morning's fills — and **6 of the 7 winners that ever filled had ALREADY FILLED when the ranking was computed** (ABCL 09:34 · BW 09:31 · ETON 09:31 · AMLX 09:32 · PHVS 09:31 · DFTX 09:33; only OKTA 09:49 came after). **Each of those six is counted TWICE — once inside `open_positions` consuming a slot, once as the winner of the slots that remain, so a flip at THIS TIMING would over-allocate. ⚠ That is an artifact of running AFTER the fills, not a sizing defect in the allocator — the timing move removes it.** The review's own comparison (audit `winners` vs actual fills) is therefore circular: agreement is guaranteed when the winners are drawn from names already filled. ⚠ Four months of telemetry answer nothing, and more days will not help — winner trade-through is **5 of 19 (26%)**, the same ~1/6 interception rate that deferred this on 2026-05-18, and on 7 of 9 contested days FEWER names entered than there were slots (39 candidates, 19 slots, 8 entries) so the 5-position cap is not what decides these books. ▶ **THE PRECONDITION: move the shadow job 9:35 → 9:28 ET and fix the double-count, shadow-only, submits nothing, $0** — then the FCFS-vs-allocator question becomes askable for the first time. ⛔ It changes WHEN a money-path job reads the book, so it ships with the numbers and **his sign-off (THE LINE)** — like the RED-3 clamp above, it must land BEFORE any Phase-1B flip. ✅ **The move was checked before being recommended: a 9:28 job does NOT read an empty queue.** Since #515 the only writer is `enqueue_pending_allocation` (`ep_detector.py:5999`) on an EP scan that runs every 5 min from 07:00 ET, and **19 of 27 queue rows across five sampled days were already present by 09:28**. ⚠ **Its cost, stated: the other 8 arrive AFTER 09:28** — post-open upgrades from the dedicated 9:31 `ep_scan_open` job — so a 9:28 allocator ranks the field EARLY but INCOMPLETE. The richer alternative is the 2026-07-03 doc's §9.3 (read slots live at each candidate's own attempt time), which keeps the full field and costs more. ✅ **HE RULED 2026-09-14: "aligned, A, then B if this becomes necessary" — SIGNED. Do A (move the shadow job to 9:28 ET + fix the double-count), and only build B (slots read live at each candidate's own attempt time) if A's incomplete-field cost shows up in the data.** ⚠ A is money-path TIMING, so it goes in a WEEKEND build slot (Sat 2026-09-19 or Sun 09-20), not a market day; it stays shadow-only and submits nothing. ⚠ **What would make B necessary, written down now so it is not decided after the fact: the post-9:28 arrivals turn out to be the ones that WIN the slots** — concretely, if over 15 contested post-move days the names entering after 09:28 would have outranked the 9:28 winners on more than a third of those days, A is judging the wrong field and B is owed. If they rarely outrank, A is sufficient and B is closed.  ⚠ #415 (allocator telemetry) shipped the eligibility flag and cascade info but its third piece, the operator's *"slots snapshot cadence"*, shipped as ONE value at the cron tick and closed on *"all 3 fields present"* (`731ea850`) — partially shipped, closed on the narrower reading; named as context, not reopened. 🔴 **MEASURED 2026-09-15 on his question (*"how do we determine priority of slots? it's based on score right?"*) — YES, AND THE +10 THEME BONUS IS 40% OF IT.** `ep_score` -> `score_magna53` -> `composite = 0.40*setup + ...` -> the allocator's rank. **Over 141 queued rows / 31 days: the cap actually bound on 14 days, and removing the +10 changes the WINNER SET on 2 of them (14%)** — 07-31 BLZE over MPWR (moot: both skipped downstream), 08-14 ETON over VERA (**ETON entered, +$19.32**; VERA was gap-below-floor anyway). **So it is NOT inert on the ranking side — but N=1 tradeable case is not a verdict either way.** ⚠ My first pass measured 12 scan-log HIGHs and got a clean null; the ranked population is the 141 queued rows, and on the right one the answer inverts — the same artifact as the 2026-07-19 "0 upgrades in 466 rows" read. **Re-measure after A lands, on post-move contested days.** §6 of the analysis doc. Refs #44, #415, #335, `scheduler.py:6650,7688`, `cross_strategy_allocator.py:97,241,252`.
- #313 | 2026-10-27 | pending | P6 Institution-grade ops (H1→H2): #256 split (done) + replay-everything CI (#302) + cost governance (per-role LLM spend vs P&L) [b3] [ok:#419 Phase-2 milestone re-date, operator-signed 7/5] >> ✅ **COST-GOVERNANCE INCREMENT SHIPPED 2026-08-02 — spend is now ATTRIBUTABLE PER LANE, and a lane that switches on ANNOUNCES ITSELF.** Operator, after the chart-vision shadow ran unnoticed: *"we can have hidden costs that provides no value that is running and no way for us to know about in time."* ⚠ **THE COST STACK WAS NOT THE FAILURE** — the #377 meter, the #378 board/alarm and the #379 per-caller anomaly + reduction detectors all worked; they were reading a bucket with TWO THINGS IN IT. `grade_holistic`'s `log_caller` had a **DEFAULT** of `ep_grade_judge`; the chart shadow took it, and **336 of the 379 calls in that bucket over 30 days ($12.90) were an experiment indistinguishable from live grading.** The dollars were always derivable — the ATTRIBUTION was not. `judge_divergence` had overridden the default correctly and the docstring explained why, so convention existed, was documented, and the next lane still didn't follow it. **(1) The default is GONE** — `log_caller` is REQUIRED; every lane names itself. The sweep found **8 unlabelled call sites, not 1** — every offline eval and replay was ALSO billing the live judge (`tape_judge_eval`, `judge_model_eval`, `judge_enrich_eval`, `judge_backfill_replay`, `selection_replay_268`, `judge_integration_probe`, `chart_axis_shadow`, `chart_axis_eval` are now distinct buckets). **(2) NEW-LANE DETECTOR** (`cost_board._new_lanes_from_rows` + the 17:52 ET watchdog): a caller with spend in the last 3d and ZERO across the trailing 30d Telegrams ONCE, ever (audit-log dedupe, the same idiom the 7/17 budget-alarm re-fire fix used). This closes a blind spot the code NAMED — `compute_caller_cost_anomalies` is cold-start gated *"so a brand-new caller can't false-positive on day 2"*, and the reduction heuristics only ask whether EXISTING spend is reducible, never whether a lane should exist. 28 tests. ⚠ **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT: a lane/purpose registry mapping each caller to what it buys** — it needs a "who consumes this" field nothing can verify, and #517 (filed TODAY) is live evidence that a registry without a forcing function becomes a pile. The weakest honest claim (*this started spending*) beats a richer one that rots. ✅ **VERIFIED-LIVE 2026-08-02 (deploy `0903575`, market-agent + execution).** Ran `compute_cost_watchdog` against REAL `api_usage` inside `apollo-market` (read-only — only the 17:52 ET job writes): clean run, 0 anomalies / 3 opportunities / **0 new lanes**, and CROSS-CHECKED against the raw table independently of the detector — the 5 most-recently-first-seen callers are all 6-17 days old, so zero is the CORRECT answer, not a silent failure. `judge_divergence` (first seen 7/27) is the worked example: it would have been announced on day one had this existed. ▶ **DEFERRED TO A WEEKDAY (flagged by the 8/02 /simplify altitude pass, NOT applied — money path, Sunday night, live judge grades at 09:45 ET):** the fix landed one layer too HIGH. `grade_holistic` now requires `log_caller`, but the true chokepoint is `judge_transport.invoke_forced_tool` — the function that actually calls `spend_tracker.log_anthropic_call_safe` — which still has `log_caller: Optional[str] = None`. **The residual hazard is WORSE than the one fixed**: a future forced-tool judge built directly on the transport (not via `grade_holistic`) would log NOTHING — no bucket at all, rather than the wrong bucket — and the AST test can't see it, because it hardcodes the three judge entry-point names. Verified: BOTH production call sites (`ep_grade_judge`, `mgmt_judge`) already pass a literal, so requiring it is zero-behaviour-change. ⚠ It is NOT free: `None = no logging` is a documented signed contract (#377, 'byte-identical to the pre-#377 path') with a test pinning it (`test_judge_logs_nothing_when_log_caller_none`), so this is a CONTRACT change, not a cleanup — it needs the deliberate deploy window, not a late-Sunday push. ⏭ ALSO CONSIDERED AND SKIPPED: moving the dedupe query into `db.py` per the CLAUDE.md single-SoT-for-queries rule. Two reviewers independently said leave it — `cost_board.py` already holds 3 inline cost-specific queries, and moving only the newest would split cost queries across two files, which is worse than the inconsistency it fixes. ▶ REMAINDER of #313 unchanged: replay-everything CI (#302) + spend-vs-P&L attribution.
